Cost Proposal for: Stanley Consultants 
Project: Shumway Road over Silver Creek, Bridge Replacement – ADOT LPA 
Date: March 3, 2026 
 
 
PROJECT UNDERSTANDING 
 
It is Tierra Right of Way Services, Ltd. (“Tierra”) understands that Stanley Consultants, (Client), on behalf 
of Navajo County, AZ , through the Arizona Department of Transportation ( ADOT) Local Public Agency 
(LPA) Program. would like to procure Tierra’s se rvices for the Shumway Road over Silver Creek, Bridge 
Replacement Project (Project). Ultimately, the goal of the project is to replace the bridge and realign1,000’ 
of the roadway to the South, and grade the channel North and South of the new box culvert to be installed. 
All in an effort to mitigate and control ongoing flooding that occurs along this roadway. At this time Client 
has identified to Tierra that there are four (4) parcels affected by the project that will require a permanent 
fee right of way acquisition, along with the potential for some temporary rights as well. However, due to 
common/contiguous ownerships, we believe there will be three (3) acquisitions total, to acquire all four 
(4) of the identified parcels.  Client has advised that there are Federal Funds involved with the project , 
Tierra makes it a common practice to follow the Uniform Relocation and Real Property Acquisition Policies 
Act of 1970, as amended, (URA) , unless otherwise directed by Client . Within the scope of this proposal, 
Tierra has identified costs for the services to be provided by Tierra, those services are as follows: 
 
• Task 1: Project Administration and Project Management (Tierra) 
• Task 2: Title Reports (Security Title Agency) 
• Task 3: Appraisal Reports (Landpro Valuation) 
• Task 4: Review Appraisal Reports (First Appraisal) 
• Task 5: Acquisition Services (Tierra) 
 
 
SCOPE OF WORK 
 
Task 1. Project Administration and Project Management: 
Tierra will support the Client by providing real estate services for the Project. 
 
The activities Tierra will perform under this task include the following; 
• Monthly invoicing by unit, including prior labor charges as well as the Project -to-Date labor 
charges, and reimbursable expenses. 
• Attend up to six (6) conference calls between the Client, Design Team and Tierra to discuss and 
track progress of the Project. 
• Tierra shall meet with internal staff as needed to  review the Project, and report to the Client 
accordingly of any critical issues for resolution. 
• Tierra will maintain records in accordance with all statutory, regulatory and policy requirements.  
• All acquisition parcel files will be prepared in a format that is satisfactory to the Client and will

<!-- page 53 -->
2 
 
include a standard Agent Contact Report reflecting all written, verbal and email contact with 
property owner(s). At a minimum, the acquisition parcel files will also include the title report,  
Appraisal, Review Appraisal, offer letter, escrow documents and recorded conveyance 
documents. 
• Tierra will prepare and submit progress reports , as needed, identifying the work completed by 
Tierra during the reporting period, any critical issues or problems encountered, the work to be 
completed during the next period, and status updates. 
• Tierra will prepare and submit monthly invoices to include an explanation of work completed by 
Tierra or its sub-consultants during the invoice period. 
• Tierra will utilize the Clients/Agency existing forms to prepare draft acquisition documents. 
• Tierra will prepare and provide Project tracking and status report formats. 
• Tierra will ensure proper documentation throughout the life of the project for funding 
certification. 
• Tierra will be available to meet with the Client, property owners or occupants to discuss and assist 
in resolving any issues regarding the acquisition activities for the Project. 
 
Task 1. Deliverables: 
The following items are the deliverables for this task: 
• Provide up to twelve (12) progress reports and invoices. 
• Meeting notices, agendas, and meeting notes delivered electronically to the Client for all Tierra 
led meetings.  
• Electronic parcel acquisition files that are completed and organized will be provided to the Client 
within forty-five (45) days of receipt of final title policy and recorded conveyance document(s).  
Not to exceed six (6) months from Close of Escrow. 
 
Task 1. Assumptions: 
• Client shall provide clear, concise, and appropriate guidance throughout the Project. 
 
Task 1. Schedule: 
Project Administration and Project Management activities will be provided throughout the life cycle of 
the Tierra Project. 
 
Task 2. Title Reports 
Preliminary title commitment reports will be ordered from our approved partner, Security Title Agency with 
coordination of the order and recorded documents (Schedule B exceptions) being performed by Tierra.  A title 
commitment report will be needed for each of the Fo ur (4) privately -owned parcels.  However, due to 
common/contiguous ownership, we anticipate only needing three (3) reports.   The fee for these reports is 
typically credited by the title company to the Client at close of escrow with the title comp any. Tierra 
recommends that the Client obtain title insurance and advises that for the Client to accept the right of way 
acquired, the Client’s attorney will likely require title insurance. Escrow and title insurance fees will vary based 
on the value of the land to be acquired, but not on any damages opined by the appraiser.   
Activities Tierra will perform under this task include the following; 
▪ Tierra will compile a list of the parcel numbers to be acquired for the project.  Once the list is 
compiled, Tierra will verify with Client that the parcel numbers are accurate. Once verified, Tierra 
will order the title reports from Security Title Agency.

<!-- page 54 -->
3 
 
▪ Once the title reports are received, Tierra agents will perform in-depth research of the title report 
and the Schedule B items to make sure there are no pre-existing issues that would delay the Client 
from successfully closing escrow on the properties.  If issues are noted, Tierra will bring said issues 
to Client’s attention immediately upon taking notice of the issue(s).  
 
Task 2 Deliverables: 
The following items are the deliverables for this task: 
▪ Electronic copy of the title report(s) provided to Client and a list (per parcel) noting any issues that 
may affect Client’s ability to successfully close escrow. At this time , we anticipate ordering, 
receiving and providing up to 3 title reports for the project.  
 
Task 2 Assumptions:  
Our scope of work contemplates that there will be no deviation from following assumptions relating to 
this task:  
▪ Client may request, under separate estimate, additional in-depth research on issues (if identified), 
costs are in addition to those noted herein.  
▪ Client will provide a detailed parcel count, along with parcel numbers, of the parcels it wishes for 
Tierra to order title reports for. W ork cannot commence until Client has provided said 
information. 
▪ Title Reports, (if requested) are being prepared by others. Any delay in commencement of project 
due to delay of these items shall not be Tierra’s responsibility. 
▪ If Client's lead monitor/manager changes, existing policy and procedure will not change. 
▪ The costs for survey and preparation of legal descriptions is not included within consultant's scope 
of work.  These tasks are to be completed by others, at the Client’s own separate cost and 
discretion. 
 
Task 3. Appraisals 
Tierra has selected LandPro Valuation Services to complete the appraisal reports for this project. Doug 
Estes, MAI will perform a field visit of each property to be appraised, research and analyze all supporting 
market documentation, ascertain and determin e the value of any damages and produce each appraisal 
report in conformance with USPAP and state appraisal regulations. Based on the Scope of Work 
assumptions, we have selected LandPro Valuation Services because of their experience in preparing 
complex appraisals with the potential for damage determinations and because this firm has been qualified 
by the Arizona Department of Transportation to complete such complex appraisal assignments.  
Appraisals will include an option to allow for acquisition via purchase or eminent domain.   
Activities Tierra will perform under this task include the following: 
 
▪ Tierra will order and coordinate the obtainment of three (3) appraisal reports, encompassing the 
rights needed for all four (4) parcels, from the appraiser.  Once the Appraisal reports are received 
by Tierra, we will review them in detail to make sure they are complete and accurate.

<!-- page 55 -->
4 
 
Task 3 Deliverables: 
The following items are the deliverables for this task: 
▪ Electronic copy of each completed appraisal report. If any issues or concerns are noted during 
Tierra’s review, we will make note of said issues or concerns when we transmit the appraisals to 
the client. 
 
Task 3 Assumptions:  
Our SOW presumes that there will be no deviation from the following assumptions relating to this task:  
▪ If, during ordering of reports, it is discovered that there are common/contiguous ownerships, the 
reports for those contiguous/common ownerships will be combined and will result in a net 
reduction of the total number of appraisal reports to be ordered/completed.  
▪ The costs for appraisals are flat fees set by the Appraiser. Once the Appraiser delivers the 
completed appraisal to Tierra, we will bill the client for the completed appraisal(s). Upon payment 
from the client, Tierra will pay the Appraiser for their services.  
 
Task 4. Review Appraisals 
Tierra has selected Tim Haskins of First Appraisal Services to provide appraisal review services. First 
Appraisal Services is an approved provider of review appraisal services by the Arizona Department of 
Transportation. The purpose of the review appraisal is to provide an independent review that an appraisal 
report meets USPAP, URA and state appraisal regulations. 
Note: When a government agency acquires property rights from a private property using federal funds, 
the agency is required by the Uniform Act (aka the “URA”, 49 CFR Part 24) to have the appraisal reviewed 
by an independent appraiser qualified to perform such reviews. It is paramount to provide these services 
if an agency will use its power of eminent domain to acquire any property that is not acquired through 
negotiations. To be in compliance with the URA, Tierra recommends that the services of a review appraiser 
be utilized. This will give Client piece of mind and will protect future federal funding. This task is a pre -
cursor to Task 4 below. 
Activities Tierra will perform under this task include the following; 
▪ Tierra will order and coordinate the obtainment of  three (3) review appraisal reports, 
encompassing the rights needed for all four (4) parcels requiring acquisition for the 
Project. 
▪ Once the review appraisal reports are received by Tierra, we will review them in detail to 
make sure they are complete and all accurate.  
 
Task 4 Deliverables: 
The following items are the deliverables for this task: 
▪ Electronic copy of review appraisal reports meeting USPAP, URA and state regulations identifying 
any opined differences in value and stating compliance with USPAP, URA and state regulations.

<!-- page 56 -->
5 
 
Task 4 Assumptions:  
Our scope of work contemplates that there will be no deviation from following assumptions relating to 
this task:  
▪ The costs for review appraisals are flat fees set by the appraiser.  Once the appraiser delivers the 
completed review appraisals to Tierra, we will bill Client for the completed appraisal(s). Upon 
payment from Client, Tierra will pay the appraiser for their services.   
 
Task 5. Acquisition Services 
All acquisition services will be provided directly by Tierra.  Acquisition services include the 
coordination/performance of the following items: title report, appraisal report, review appraisal report, 
preparation of all purchase agreements and conveyance documents, review of any project plans, 
negotiations with each property owner in the project limits, the maintenance of detailed contact notes 
and the coordination of activities and services stated herein . Should negotiations stall or no longer be 
viable, Tierra will make recommendations to Client to proceed with Eminent Domain so that Client may 
obtain the required property rights. If Client does not have a qualified Eminent Domain Attorney on staff, 
or retainer, Tierra can make recommendations for qualifi ed attorney’s that we work with regularly on 
these types of projects. 
Activities Tierra will perform under this task include the following; 
▪ Tierra will order and coordinate the obtainment of Three (3) title reports, appraisal 
reports and review appraisal reports. 
▪ Tierra agents will prepare the offer packages (using the Client’s approved forms) and will 
submit the completed packages to the Client for review and approval prior to making the 
offer to the property owner(s). 
▪ Tierra will present and negotiate the offers to the property owner(s). 
▪ Once an offer is accepted/signed, Tierra agents will open escrow with the title company 
and will work with title to clear any pending issues so escrow can close successfully.  
▪  Tierra agents will request funds from Client necessary to close escrow. 
▪ Once the property has closed escrow and recorded, Tierra agents will begin the file 
closeout process. 
▪ If negotiations are not successful, Tierra will make recommendations to the Client to 
proceed with Eminent Domain so that Client may secure the required property rights.  
Tierra will turn over the file and all applicable data pertaining to the file, so that Client(s) 
attorney may initiate the eminent domain proceedings. 
 
Task 5 Deliverables: 
The following items are the deliverables for this task: 
▪ Private parcel acquisition file, in hardcopy and electronic format containing all relevant 
documents, communications and plans relating to each acquisition. 
 
Task 5 Assumptions:  
Our scope of work contemplates that there will be no deviation from following assumptions relating to 
this task:  
▪ Tierra will make up to 3 attempts to negotiate in good faith with each property owner and secure 
their approval/acceptance of Client’s offer.  If an agreement can be reached/negotiated, Tierra 
will make recommendations to move to Eminent Domain, we will prepare our file and deliv er it 
to Client for initiation of those proceedings.

<!-- page 57 -->
6 
 
▪ Client will provide the standard template forms to be utilized for the project (Offer letter, 
Purchase Agreement, legal descriptions etc.). If the client does not have standard template forms, 
Tierra recommends using the ADOT standard template forms as a starting point. 
▪ If additional parcels are identified after the approval of this estimate, Tierra will be afforded the 
opportunity to revise this proposal and fee accordingly. 
▪ Client will provide standard template forms to be used on the project.  If Client does not have 
template forms, Tierra can provide said forms, tailored to Client, at an additional cost. 
▪ If negotiations stall or are no longer viable, Client will not unreasonably delay Tierra from turning 
over the file for initiation of Eminent Domain proceedings (to be handled by Client). 
▪ Should Tierra’s assistance be required on a parcel after it has been turned over for Eminent 
Domain, Tierra will bill Client at Tierra’s hourly rates (Included herein), for the actual hours 
worked. Said hours are not a part of this estimate. 
 
PROJECT SCHEDULE 
Table 1 below indicates our anticipated schedule to complete the scope of work. This schedule 
anticipates receiving Notice to Proceed no later than April 1, 2026.  
Table 1-Schedule: 
 
 
BILLING TERMS: LUMP SUM BY MILESTONE

HOURS/MILEAGE ESTIMATE

Project Manager 15 hours/acquisition x 3 acquisitions = 45 hours.

Admin   5 hours/acquisition x 3 acquisitions = 15 hours

Project and Cost Assumptions: 
Tierra’s proposed cost to provide the services contemplated in this proposal are based on the following 
standard assumptions: 
➢ Properties are to be acquired in accordance with the Uniform Act. Unless otherwise directed by Client. 
➢ If Client has eminent domain rights, Tierra will suggest that Client exercise those rights as a means to 
keep the project moving forward, (in the event negotiations have stalled or are no longer viable).  
➢ Legal Descriptions are being prepared by others. Any delay in commencement of project due to delay 
of these items shall not be Tierra’s responsibility.

<!-- page 58 -->
7 
 
➢ Title Reports, Appraisal Reports and Review Appraisal reports are being prepared by others. Any delay 
in commencement of project due to delay of these items shall not be Tierra’s responsibility. Tierra will 
provide regular updates to Client on the status of these services, and will advise if any of these services 
will cause delays in the next stage of Tierra’s scope of work. If delays arise, Client and Tierra will discuss 
resolutions in an attempt to avoid further delays. 
➢ Any review of materials required by the Client and produced by Tierra for an acquisition will be done 
in a timely manner and shall not exceed 5 business days. (Relocations are not anticipated at this time, 
however, should relocations come into play, Tierra reserves the right to amend this fee accordingly 
to cover those services). 
➢ If Client's lead monitor/manager changes, existing policy and procedure will not change. 
➢ Legal descriptions and exhibits will be delivered to Tierra (per parcel), prior to appraisals being 
requested. 
➢ The costs for survey and preparation of legal descriptions are not included within consultant's scope 
of work. 
➢ The costs for escrow closing services are not included within consultant's scope of work. 
➢ Tierra's cost assumes a 30-60 day negotiation period to successfully complete each acquisition. If an 
agreement is not reached within the 30 –60-day timeframe, Tierra will close the file and deliver to 
Client for initiation of Eminent Domain proceedings.   
➢ Once a file has been transmitted to the Client for condemnation, any further requests for information 
from the Client's attorney shall be invoiced at the current hourly billing rate for the Tierra employee 
providing the information. 
➢ Project billing is to be Lump Sum by Milestone. Milestone’s are listed in the proposal. 
➢ Title reports, appraisals and review appraisal preparation fees will be invoiced upon receipt from the 
parties preparing said reports. Said billing will be billed as incurred and may not coincide with Tierra’s 
milestone billing(s).

Title: Contract for Janitorial Services - Taylor Complex

Approval of Contract for Janitorial Services for the Navajo County Complex located at 1121 W. Pinedale
Road, Taylor, with WCD Enterprises, LLC
Background:
Navajo County currently requires a contractor to provide janitorial services at the Navajo County
Complex located at 1121 W. Pinedale Road, Taylor, Arizona.  The janitorial services to be provided
under this contract include cleaning the Taylor Complex, a 10,393 square foot building, at the
above-noted location two times per week, during regular business hours, which are Monday through
Friday, 7:00 a.m. to 5:00 p.m., with the specific days of the week to be agreed upon by County and
Contactor.

CONTRACT FOR JANITORIAL SERVICES BETWEEN NAVAJO COUNTY AND WCD ENTERPRISES 
THIS CONTRACT is made and entered into this ____ day of 2026, by and between Navajo County, a 
poliƟcal subdivision of the State of Arizona, hereinaŌer referred to as the “County”, and WCD 
Enterprises, LLC, hereinaŌer referred to as “Contractor”.  
WHEREAS, Navajo County currently requires a contractor to provide janitorial services at the Navajo 
County Complex located at 1121 W. Pinedale Road, Taylor Arizona, and Contractor represents that 
Contractor has the experƟse and is qualiﬁed to perform the janitorial services needed by the County and 
as described in this Contract, 
NOW THEREFORE, in consideraƟon of the mutual promises and obligaƟons set forth herein, the parƟes 
hereto agree as follows:  
1.  Scope of Work:    The janitorial services to be provided under this contract include cleaning the 
Navajo County Taylor Complex, a 10,393 square foot building, at the above noted locaƟon two Ɵmes per 
week, during regular business hours which are Monday through Friday, 7:00 a.m. to 5:00 p.m.  The 
speciﬁc days of the week the services shall be provided shall be agreed upon by County and Contactor, 
but in no event will the days be consecuƟve.  DeviaƟon from the agreed upon schedule may occur with 
prior wriƩen approval from the County.   All work performed under this Agreement shall be performed in 
a skillful and workmanlike manner.  The speciﬁc janitorial services included are: 
      a.)  Sweeping and wet-mopping all hard surface ﬂoors; 
 b.)  Vacuum all carpeted areas, including any rugs; 
c.)  Trash removal and liner replacement as necessary (when they are soiled, torn or worn).  
County will provide an on-site dumpster where Contractor may dispose of trash; 
 d.)  Cleaning of o ﬃce glass parƟƟons (as needed); 
 e.)  Light dus Ɵng of accessible surfaces (as allowed) and periodic dusƟng of window blinds; 
f.)  Restroom cleaning and paper products and soap dispensers reﬁlled as needed, including 
sweeping and mopping of ﬂoors, cleaning toilets, inside and out, cleaning and saniƟzing shower 
stalls, sinks, countertops, ﬁxtures and mirrors; 
 g.)  Cleaning and polish drinking fountain; 
 h.)  Cleaning counters, sinks, tables and chairs in the break room and conference room; 
 i.)  keeping the janitorial closet clean and organized. 
1.2  Materials provided to Contractor by County:   Navajo County shall provide the Contractor with the 
following items, all of which will be stocked and stored in the janitorial room: 
 a.)  Hand soap for all soap dispensers; 
 b.)  Paper towels; 
 c.)  Toilet paper;

<!-- page 95 -->
2 
 
 d.)  Trash can liners; 
 e.)  Toilet seat covers; 
 f.)  Feminine hygiene disposal liners; 
 g.)  Dispenser replacements as necessary. 
 Contractor shall no Ɵfy County FaciliƟes staﬀ when supplies are running low. 
1.3  Materials Provided by Contractor:   Contractor shall provide all labor necessary to complete the 
tasks detailed herein.   The materials which shall be furnished by Contractor include: 
a.)  All cleaning chemicals along with an MSDS for all cleaning chemicals Contractor may choose 
to store on the premises; 
 b.)  Mops; 
 c.)  Mop buckets 
 d.)  Brooms; 
 e.)  Vacuums; 
f.)  Any other tools deemed necessary to complete the cleaning scope of work as deﬁned in this 
contract. 
1.4  Restricted Spaces:   Approximately 1,340 square feet of the Navajo County Taylor Complex is 
“restricted space” that the contractor will not be obligated to clean under the terms of this agreement.   
The restricted areas include: 
a.)  Sheriﬀ’s SubstaƟon, Evidence Room.    Access is not permiƩed to the Contractor, and this 
area will be restricted via card reader access restricƟons as well as through lock and key 
restricƟons. 
b.)  Health Department.   The Contractor will be prohibited access to the Exam Room, Walk-In 
Cooler, X-Ray Room, Histology Room and Medical Examiner’s Storage.  Access is not permiƩed to 
the Contractor, and this area will be restricted via card reader access restricƟons as well as 
through lock and key restricƟons. 
c.)  Locked Doors:   If any doors within the facility are locked when cleaning crews are on-site, it 
is understood by the Contractor that space will not be cleaned that day. 
1.5  Quality Control:  If Navajo County contacts Contractor with speciﬁc requests or concerns, Contractor 
shall respond immediately to the Facility and make any necessary correcƟons to resolve the issue. 
2.  Fee:  County shall pay contractor the sum of $1,195.00 per month for the services rendered under 
this Contract.   Contractor shall bill the county for the services rendered pursuant to this Contract at the 
end of every month, and said invoices shall be paid by the County within twenty days of receipt. 
Contractor is an independent contractor, and shall be solely legally responsible for any and all tax 
obligaƟons which may result out of Contractor’s performance of this Contract. County shall have no legal

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. o.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2026  
Title: Letter of Support for Little Colorado Medical Center
Submitted By: Leah Thomas, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Motion before the Board:
Letter of Support for the Little Colorado Medical Center's proposal for a residency program
Background:
Residency programs play a vital role in addressing physician shortages by training future physicians in
the communities where they are most needed. Studies consistently demonstrate that physicians are more
likely to practice near the location of their residency training. By creating opportunities for graduate
medical education in northeastern Arizona, Little Colorado Medical Center will help build a sustainable
pipeline of healthcare professionals committed to serving rural populations.
Attachments
Letter of Support - Little Colorado Medical Center 
Form Review
Inbox Reviewed By Date
Clerk of the Board (Originator) Leah Thomas 06/12/2026 08:41 AM
Form Started By: Leah Thomas Started On: 06/11/2026 10:50 AM
Final Approval Date: 06/12/2026

<!-- page 104 -->
Fern Benally • Alberto L. Peshlakai • Jason E. Whiting • Daryl Seymore • Dawnafe 
Whitesinger 
928-524-4053
100 East Code Talkers Drive, Holbrook, AZ 
86025
www.navajocountyaz.gov
     NAVAJO COUNTY
    BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
June 23, 2026
To Whom It May Concern:
On behalf of Navajo County, I am pleased to express our strong support for the 
establishment and advancement of the residency program at Little Colorado Medical 
Center .
Navajo County serves a large and geographically diverse population, many of whom 
reside in rural and medically underserved communities. Ensuring access to quality 
healthcare remains one of our highest priorities. The development of a residency 
training program at Little Colorado Medical Center represents a significant opportunity 
to strengthen the healthcare workforce, improve access to care, and enhance the overall 
health of residents throughout our county and the surrounding region.
Residency programs play a vital role in addressing physician shortages by training future 
physicians in the communities where they are most needed. Studies consistently 
demonstrate that physicians are more likely to practice near the location of their 
residency training. By creating opportunities for graduate medical education in 
northeastern Arizona, Little Colorado Medical Center will help build a sustainable 
pipeline of healthcare professionals committed to serving rural populations.
In addition to improving healthcare access, the residency program will provide 
meaningful economic and community benefits. The program will attract talented 
medical professionals, support local employment, encourage collaboration among 
healthcare and educational institutions, and contribute to the long-term vitality of our 
region.
Navajo County appreciates the leadership and commitment demonstrated by Little 
Colorado Medical Center in pursuing this important initiative. We are confident that the 
residency program will have a lasting positive impact on the health and well-being of our 
residents and will strengthen healthcare services throughout the county.
We respectfully offer our full support for this endeavor and encourage favorable 
consideration of any applications, accreditations, or funding requests associated with the 
residency program.

<!-- page 105 -->
Navajo County
Date
2
Sincerely,
Daryl Seymore
Chairman
Navajo County Board of Supervisors

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. p.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2026  
Title: Residential Substance Abuse Treatment Grant Agreement
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Motion before the Board:
Approval of a grant award from the Arizona Criminal Justice Commission, pass-through entity for the
Bureau of Justice Assistance
Background:
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office has received a third cycle of funding from the Residential Substance
Abuse Treatment (RSAT) formula grant program, which enhances the capabilities of state, local, and
tribal governments to provide residential substance use disorder (SUD) treatment to adult and juvenile
populations during detention or incarceration. The RSAT program encourages the establishment and
maintenance of drug-free prisons and jails and the development and implementation of specialized
residential SUD treatment for individuals with co-occuring mental health and substance-use
disorders.The Office was awarded $75,165.17 for the salary and fringe benefits of its Program
Coordinator. This grant requires a match of $18,791.29 which will be funded from opioid settlement
monies. Effective 7/1/2026 - 6/30/2027.

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. q.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2026  
Title: Robert Santos Contract Amendment #2
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Motion before the Board:
Approval of the Robert Santos Contract Amendment #2 for Law Enforcement Services for Navajo
County Sheriff's Office
Background:
Robert Santos is an Independent Contractor assigned to the Norlhem Arizona Law Enforcement Training
Academy (NALETA) as aRecruit Training Officer (RTO). His duties consist of the development of new
police recruits by coordinating training, coordinating instructors to teach, filling in as needed for
instructors who were not available to instruct, writing AZPOST approved exams for the recruit class,
administering the exams, grading of exams, tracking, and inputting results, grading recruits for Northern
Pioneer College credits and forwarding the grades to Northern Pioneer College for credit. Additionally,he
is responsible for conducting physical fitness sessions, mentoring, and instruction in various law
enforcement subjects.

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. r.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2026  
Title: Navajo County Vehicle Request
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Motion before the Board:
Approval of vehicle donation request by the Northeastern Arizona Training Center
Background:
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office requests authorization from the Board of Supervisors to donate two
(2) retired law enforcement vehicles to the Northern Arizona Regional Law Enforcement Training
Academy. The vehicles have reached the end of their useful service life for Sheriff's Office operations
and are no longer considered roadworthy for law enforcement use. Due to their age, mileage, condition,
and anticipated repair costs, the vehicles have minimal market value and is not suitable for continued
fleet service.
The Law Enforcement Training Academy has expressed interest in receiving the vehicles for training
purposes. The vehicles would be utilized in practical training exercises, including vehicle stop scenarios,
emergency response training, and other law enforcement instructional activities. Donation of the vehicles
will provide a public benefit by supporting the training and development of future law enforcement
officers while avoiding disposal costs to the County.
Therefore, the Navajo County Sheriff's Office requests Board approval to declare the vehicle surplus
property and authorize their donation to the Northern Arizona Regional Law Enforcement Training
Academy for training purposes.
Attachments
NALETA Vehicle Request

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. s.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2026  
Title: Naming two existing unnamed roads in the “Clay Springs” area to “Ironstone Road (East –
West) & Red Tail Way (North – South)” in Section 15, Township 11 North - Range 19 East
in District IV.
Submitted For: William Flake, County Engineer 
Submitted By: Julianna Davis, Hydrologly Technician 
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Naming two existing unnamed roads in the “Clay Springs” area to “Ironstone Road (East – West) & Red
Tail Way (North – South)” in Section 15, Township 11 North - Range 19 East in District IV
Background:
The District IV roads are located within Section 15, Township 11 North – Range 19 East.
Easement: Ironstone Road (East – West) & portion of Red Tail Way (North – South) were granted per
Recorded Deed 2024-09659.
Portion of Red Tail Way (North – South) was granted per Recorded Deed 2026-03080 & Survey
2024-10053. Please refer to the attached items for more details.
Purpose of Naming: This road naming is necessary to ensure accurate addressing and reliable Emergency
Services/E911 response to the properties located along this access corridor. Due to the distance from
Rocky Lane and the number of properties relying on this route, an official road name is required to
support public safety and proper location identification.
Road names: Ironstone Road & Red Tail Way were the names selected by Navajo County. Three affected
property owners were notified via certified mail; two notices were successfully delivered, and one was
returned undeliverable. The two recipients who received the notifications expressed no objections to the
proposed road names.
This is not County-maintained.

Board of Supervisors Regular 4. a. 1.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2026  
Title: CTR061423 Amendment 5 COVID-19 Expansion Project
Submitted For: Janelle Linn, Health Director 
Submitted By: Ciera Hensley, Deputy Health Director
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Motion before the Board:
Approval of the Intergovernmental Agreement CTR061423 Amendment 5 between the Arizona
Department of Health Services and the Navajo County Public Health Services District for the COVID-19
Expansion IGA
Background:
The Navajo County Public Health Services District has historically faced funding limitations in
developing and sustaining a comprehensive infectious disease surveillance program. These challenges
were further underscored during the COVID-19 pandemic. In March 2021, the County was awarded
funding to support the development, enhancement, and sustainability of this program, with funding
extended through July 31, 2026.
This contract amendment reallocates existing grant funds from the Additional Project Costs budget
category to the Indirect Costs, Supplies, and Professional and Outside Services categories. The
reallocation is necessary to align the budget with program expenditures and ensure adequate funding is
available to support ongoing grant activities through the remainder of the grant period. No changes are
proposed to the scope of work or total budget. All costs incurred will be covered by the IGA.

Board of Supervisors Regular 4. a. 2.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2026  
Title: CTR064801 A 3 PHIG
Submitted For: Janelle Linn, Health Director 
Submitted By: Ciera Hensley, Deputy Health Director
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Motion before the Board:
Approval of the Intergovernmental Agreement CTR064801 Amendment 3 between the Arizona
Department of Health Services and the Navajo County Public Health Services District for the Public
Health Infrastructure Grant (PHIG) IGA
Background:
This workforce development funding was awarded to support the recruitment, retention, training, and
overall capacity of the Navajo County public health workforce. Through internal assessment, the
department identified a significant operational gap related to the workload placed on the Finance
Manager. The scope and complexity of financial responsibilities necessitated additional support to
maintain efficiency, strengthen fiscal oversight, and ensure sustainable financial management practices.
With this funding, the health department hired and trained an Account Specialist to assist with financial
operations. The addition of this position has significantly improved workflow efficiency, strengthened
internal financial controls, and enhanced the department’s ability to manage funding in a transparent and
accountable manner. This support has also enabled the development and enhancement of financial
processes designed to reduce audit findings, improve fiscal transparency, and provide financial guidance
and training to program managers. Overall, this investment has increased workforce capacity and
strengthened the department’s financial infrastructure.
The current amendment includes minimal changes. Asterisks have been added to the budget line items
that are included in the indirect cost calculation. No changes have been made to the scope of work or
budget.

Title: Child Care Health Consultant Grant Annual Contract Renewal
GRA-STATE-24-1245-01-Y4
Submitted By: Deborah Huish, Program Manager
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Motion before the Board:
Approval of the Child Care Health Consultation Contract Number GRA-STATE-24-1245-01-Y4 with
First Things First for Fiscal Year 2027 Annual Contract Renewal
Background:
The Child Care Health Consultant strategy works with early care and education (ECE) administrative and
teaching staff at child care centers and homes to increase their knowledge and implementation of health
and safety practices. The program is grant-funded through First Things First and provides services to four
regions: Navajo/Apache Region, the White Mountain Apache Tribe Region, the San Carlos Apache Tribe
Region and the Navajo Nation Region. The program is in its twelfth year with the Navajo County Public
Health Services District; this current grant document as written would provide services for up to 54 child
care sites.

Administrative/Indirect Costs:  Administrative costs are general or centralized expenses of overall 
administration of an agency/organization that receives grant funds and does not include particular program 
costs.  Such costs are generally identified with the agency/organization’s overall operation and are further

<!-- page 160 -->
Page | 6 
described in 2 CFR 220, 2 CFR 225, and 2 CFR 230.
Indirect costs are costs of an organization that are not readily assignable to a particular program, but are 
necessary to the operation of the organization and the performance of the program.  The cost of operating and 
maintaining facilities, depreciation, and administrative salaries are examples of the types of costs that are 
usually treated as indirect.
Applicants must list either Option A or Option B and provide proper justification for expenses included:

Board of Supervisors Regular 4. a. 4.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2026  
Title: CTR063750 Amendment 5 Arizona's Prescription Drug Overdose Prevention Program
Submitted For: Janelle Linn, Health Director 
Submitted By: Amy Stradling, Department Manager
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Motion before the Board:
 Approval of Amendment No. 5 to Intergovernmental Agreement CTR063750 Arizona's Prescription
Drug Overdose Prevention Program between the Arizona Department of Health Services and the Navajo
County Public Health Services District
Background:
This amendment pertains to our existing State Opioid Response (SOR) Grant currently administered by
the Navajo County Public Health Services District. The SOR Grant is an initiative that supports local
efforts to prevent opioid-related overdoses, expand access to treatment and recovery services, and
strengthen community-based responses to substance-use disorders.
The amendment includes minor revisions to the Scope of Work, primarily removing references to
partnering entities and making minor adjustments to budget line-item allocations. No significant changes
to the overall grant funding, program objectives, service delivery, or anticipated community impact are
proposed.
Through this funding, Navajo County facilitates a multidisciplinary Overdose Fatality Review Team that
examines overdose deaths to identify contributing factors, service gaps, and opportunities for system
improvement. The grant also supports the implementation and maintenance of a case management system
designed to enhance coordination among service providers, improve referrals and follow-up, and
strengthen support for individuals affected by substance use disorders. These activities contribute to the
county's ongoing efforts to reduce overdose-related morbidity and mortality while improving access to
prevention, treatment, and recovery resources.

1. BACKGROUND 
 
1.1. The Arizona Department of Health Services (ADHS), through the Office of Injury and Violence Prevention 
(OIVP), administers funding provided by the Arizona Health Care Cost Containment System (AHCCCS) to 
operate the State Opioid Response (SOR) program. The primary objective of SOR funding is to assist Arizona 
in strengthening local capacity within counties to develop Overdose Fatality Review (OFR) teams. These teams 
bring together community agencies in a structured process to systematically share information on overdose 
events, identify risk factors, and develop actionable prevention strategies.  
 
1.2. The abuse and addiction to opioids remain critical public health challenges both nationally and within Arizona. 
Drug overdose deaths have steadily increased over the past two decades, now representing the leading cause 
of injury -related death in the United States. According to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention 
(CDC), overdose deaths surged to 92,452 in 2020—a thirty percent (30%) rise from 71,130 deaths in 2019—
with opioids implicated in 69,031 of these cases, accounting for seventy -five percent (75%) of all overdose 
fatalities. 
 
1.3. Historically, the opioid epidemic was driven largely by prescription medications. Arizona’s Controlled 
Substances Prescription Monitoring Program (CSPMP) data show that in 2019, approximately 4.1 million Class 
II-IV prescriptions were written, resulting in the dispensation of over 240 million controlled substance pills 
statewide. This equates to around thirty-four (34) Schedule II-IV controlled substance pills per resident. Recent 
prescribing practices rank Arizona as twenty-eighth (28th) nationally for opioid prescriptions, with a rate of 44.1 
prescriptions per 100 people, indicating that prescriptions are no longer the primary cause of overdose deaths. 
 
1.4. Currently, fentanyl has become the leading driver of the opioid crisis. In 2019, synthetic opioids, mainly 
fentanyl, contributed to over 36,000 deaths in the United States, representing about seventy -three percent 
(73%) of all opioid -related fatalities that year. Most fentanyl -related deaths involve illicitly manufactured 
fentanyl, often found in counterfeit pills or combined with other drugs, such as heroin or methamphetamine. In 
Arizona, fentanyl’s presence in overdos e cases climbed significantly from nine percent (9%) in 2017 to fifty 
percent (50%) in 2021. 
 
1.5. The financial toll of opioid misuse is substantial as well. In 2019, Arizona experienced 56,623 opioid- related 
hospital visits, costing an average of $11,942.00 per visit —totaling approximately $676 million in healthcare 
expenses attributed to opioids.  
 
1.6. The escalating addiction rates and overdose deaths related to both prescription and illicit opioids underscore 
a growing national crisis. In Arizona, overdose fatalities are now the leading cause of preventable injury deaths, 
underscoring the urgency for interventions. ADHS is committed to collaborating with County health 
departments to build systems and enhance capacity to address substance misuse and abuse through  
community-based case management initiatives, thus fostering improved public health outcomes statewide. 
 
1.7. Navajo County Public Health Services District (NCPHSD) plans to use State Opioid Response (SOR) funds to 
establish and support local Overdose Fatality Review (OFR) teams, which aim to bring together community 
organizations and agencies in a structured process to review overdose deaths. These OFR teams analyze 
individual overdose events to identify risk factors and trends, enabling the development of targeted prevention 
strategies. Additionally, NCPHSD intends to use SOR funds to strengthen local partnerships, improve data-
sharing capacities, and implement prevention and intervention strategies that address opioid misuse and 
addiction, especially focusing on the rising impact of fentanyl in the community. Through these efforts, 
NCPHSD aims to reduce overdose deaths, enhance community education on opioid risks, and provide 
supportive resources to individuals and families affected by opioid misuse. 
 
 
 
2. OBJECTIVE

2.1. The objective of this Agreement is to enhance the County Health Departments' capacity to combat the opioid 
crisis by establishing local drug Overdose Fatality Review (OFR) teams and deploying community health 
workers, case managers, first respo nders, and peer navigators to support high- risk populations, improving 
access to care and reducing opioid misuse across communities. 
 
3. SCOPE OF SERVICE 
 
3.1. The purpose of this initiative is to strengthen NCPHSD overdose prevention efforts through systematic reviews, 
data collection, and linkages to care (LtC). NCPHSD shall designate a point of contact responsible for 
conducting systematic, multidisciplinary, and multimodal reviews of drug overdose fatalities to identify 
actionable, locally-implementable prevention recommendations. 
 
3.2. NCPHSD shall request and gather records for each case, including medical records, toxicology and medical 
examiner reports, behavioral health records, criminal justice records, prescription drug history records from the 
CSPMP, Department of Child Services records, Emergency Medical Services/Fire Department records, and 
next of kin interviews, when applicable. Based on these records, NCPHSD shall use the designated data tool 
spreadsheet to document case demographics, methods of i njury, substance use history, behavioral health 
history, healthcare utilization, stressors, childhood history, and chronic conditions relevant to each OFR case. 
All information from gathered records shall be entered into the data tool spreadsheet, and the completed 
document shall be submitted to the OFR Epidemiologist at ADHS. NCPHSD shall respond to feedback from 
the OFR Epidemiologist to ensure all data aligns with standards for annual data analysis and statewide 
reporting. 
 
3.3. Establishing LtC, a representative from NCPHSD shall actively participate in the ADHS LtC workgroup to 
promote community-based support for those impacted by opioid misuse. 
 
3.4. NCPHSD shall develop or enhance case management systems to help individuals navigate pathways to care, 
focusing on identifying the needs of incarcerated individuals during assessments and follow -up sessions. 
NCPHSD shall match individuals with local or the nearest facilities to ensure access to necessary treatment 
and initiate discharge planning. Care shall be coordi nated with inpatient and outpatient behavioral health 
services, probation and court programs, and health plans. Additionally, NCPHSD shall maintain a 
transportation program to support a “warm hand-off” transition for individuals upon release. 
 
3.5. Through these comprehensive efforts, NCPHSD aims to improve overdose prevention, promote  life- saving 
overdose prevention and response services, and provide vital community -based care linkages for those 
impacted by the opioid crisis. 
 
4. REQUIREMENTS 
 
The County shall: 
 
4.1. Participate in surveys, interviews (remote or face-to-face), and questionnaires developed and disseminated by 
ADHS’ Evaluation Team or Consultant to collect data and information necessary to assess the state and local 
progress with meeting grant related goals, objectives, evaluation, and outcomes. 
 
4.2. Prepare and submit annual budget(s) and work/ action plan(s).  
 
4.3. Prepare and submit quarterly Contractors Expenditures Reports (CERs) with documentation. 
 
4.4. Submit quarterly reports to ADHS detailing quarterly progress on funded activities.  
 
4.5. Attend and participate in quarterly Contractor meetings with ADHS. 
4.6. Assign at least one staff person to attend and participate in ADHS’ LtC workgroup.

5. FUNDING RESTRICTIONS 
 
In addition to 45 CFR Part 75, Subpart E’s guidance regarding allowable/unallowable expenditures, Substance 
Abuse and Mental Health Services Administration (SAMHSA) funds shall not be used to:   
 
5.1. Purchase, prescribe, or provide marijuana or treatment using marijuana. See, e.g., 45 CFR.75.300(a) 
(requiring Health and Human Services (HHS) to ensure that Federal funding is expended in full accordance 
with U.S. statutory and public policy requirements); 21 U.S.C. 812(c)(10) and 841 (prohibiting the possession, 
manufacture, sale, purchase, or distribution of marijuana).   
 
5.2. Purchase, procure, or distribute pipes or cylindrical objects intended to be used to smoke or inhale illegal 
scheduled substances.   
 
5.3. Pay for promotional items including, but not limited to, clothing and commemorative items, such as pens, 
mugs/cups, folders/folios, lanyards, and conference bags. (45 CFR 75.421(e)(3))   
 
5.4. Pay for the purchase or construction of any building or structure to house any part of the program. Minor 
alterations and renovations (A&R) shall be authorized for up to twenty -five percent (25%) of a given budget 
period or $150,000.00 (whichever is less) for existing facilities, if necessary and appropriate to the project . 
Minor A&R may not include a structural change (e.g., to the foundation, roof, floor, or exterior or loadbearing 
walls of a facility, or extension of an existing facility) to achieve the following: increase the floor area; and/or, 
change the function and purpose of the facility. SAMHSA & AHCCCS must approve all minor A&R.   
 
5.5. Provide inpatient treatment or hospital-based detoxification services. Residential services are not considered 
to be inpatient or hospital-based services.   
 
5.6. Pay for housing other than recovery housing, which includes application fees and security deposits.   
 
5.7. Make direct payments to individuals to enter treatment or continue to participate in prevention or treatment 
services. (See 42 U.S.C. § 1320a-7b).   
 
5.7.1. A recipient or treatment or prevention provider may provide up to $25.00 non -cash incentive (for 
example, gift cards, bus passes, or gifts) to individuals to participate in required data collection follow-
up. This amount may be paid for participation in ea ch required data collection follow -up interview. 
Incentives cannot be provided for completing an intake or exit interview. For programs including 
contingency management as a component of the treatment program, each individual contingency 
must be $15.00 or less in value and clients may not receive contingencies totaling more than $75.00 
per budget period.   
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
5.8. Purchase firearms.

9. DELIVERABLES 
 
The County shall provide: 
 
9.1. A complete annual OFR data collection tool using the template provided by ADHS.  
 
9.1.1. A complete data tool shall entail: 
 
9.1.1.1. A death certificate number for each case. 
 
9.1.1.2. At least one (1) standardized prevention recommendation for each case reviewed. 
 
9.1.1.3. Each case has no more than four (4) columns with missing or “unknown” responses. 
 
9.1.1.4. No missing data in required columns. 
 
9.1.1.5. All cases submitted follow the State’s case requirements of at least eighteen (18) years 
of age, not pregnant in the last year, Arizona resident, and not a suicide death. 
 
9.1.1.6. Notation of medical records received for each case, including facility or provider 
requested from, date request sent, and date records received. 
 
9.2. State Overdose Response (SOR) Grant Deliverables Timeline (September 30 – September 29)

9.3. A complete annual report sent to the ADHS OFR Program Manager by July 1st.

Board of Supervisors Regular 7. a.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2026  
Title: Retirement of K-9 Sejn
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Presented By: Lt. Daniel Deets
Subject:
Celebration of the retirement of K-9 Sejn.
Background: 
Today, we would like to recognize the service and retirement of Detention K-9 Sejn.
Sejn began his law enforcement career with the Arizona Department of Public Safety. In 2021, following
changes to Arizona's marijuana laws, DPS retired Sejn at the age of six. However, it quickly became
apparent that Sejn was not ready to hang up his leash. Recognizing his drive and desire to continue
working, DPS reached out to the Navajo County Sheriff's Office to determine whether he might be a good
fit for our agency.
After evaluating Sejn, NCSO personnel determined that he would be an excellent addition to our
Detention K-9 program. While he was primarily assigned to detention operations rather than full-time
street deployment, Sejn played a critical role in maintaining the safety and security of our jail facilities.
His presence served as a powerful deterrent to those attempting to introduce contraband into the jail, and
he also assisted with numerous callouts and enforcement operations throughout Navajo County.
Within our K-9 Unit, Sejn earned the nickname "Benjamin Button." Despite coming out of retirement and
being older than many of the other working dogs, he consistently displayed the energy, enthusiasm, and
work ethic of a much younger K-9. His dedication allowed him to serve an additional five years,
extending his working career well beyond what is typical for many law enforcement dogs.
During his service with the Navajo County Sheriff's Office, Sejn was responsible for the seizure of:
• 138.7 pounds of methamphetamine
• 28 pounds of marijuana
• 18,573 fentanyl pills
• 8 PCP blotters
• 1.58 ounces of crack cocaine
These seizures represent dangerous drugs removed from our communities and reflect the significant
contribution Sejn made to public safety in Navajo County.
As happens to all of us, Father Time has finally caught up with Sejn, and the time has come for him to
enjoy a well-earned retirement. On behalf of the Navajo County Sheriff's Office and the citizens we
serve, we extend our sincere gratitude for his years of dedicated service and wish him many happy and

<!-- page 185 -->
serve, we extend our sincere gratitude for his years of dedicated service and wish him many happy and
comfortable years ahead.
Thank you, Sejn, for a job exceptionally well done.

K-9 Sejn

<!-- page 187 -->
Board of Supervisors Regular 7. b.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2026  
Title: Retirement of K-9 Zolton
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Presented By: Lt. Daniel Deets
Subject:
Celebration of the retirement of K-9 Zolton.
Background: 
Today, the Navajo County Sheriff's Office is honored to recognize the distinguished service and
retirement of K-9 Zolton.
K9 Zolton, a Belgian Malinois, came to the Navajo County Sheriff’s Office in October 2017, originally
assigned to Deputy Randy Keith and later reassigned to now Lieutenant Danny Deets in November 2019.
During his almost 9-year career with NCSO, Zolton leaves the following seizure legacy: 
5 criminal apprehensions without the use of a bite
Seizure of 89.4 pounds of cocaine
Seizure of 40.3 pounds of fentanyl powder
Seizure of 757,330 fentanyl pills
Seizure of 18 pounds of heroin
Seizure of 1,819.6 pounds of marijuana
Seizure of 1,316 THC vape pens
Seizure of 600.4 pounds of methamphetamine
Seizure of 600 ecstasy pills
Seizure of 54 LSD blotters
Seizure of $220,339 in U.S. currency
His successful drug finds were often featured on social media, and Zolton gained popularity online under
the hashtag #TeamZolton.
While most dogs are highly motivated by play, Zolton is highly motivated by his work. Throughout his
career with NCSO, he demonstrated a continual dedication to detection and protection, the true definition
of a police dog.
In his retirement, we hope Zolton will find as much satisfaction in chasing his favorite toy as he has in
chasing down criminal activity. We speak for the entire Sheriff’s Office when we say, “Good boy,
Zolton.”

Board of Supervisors Regular 10. a.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2026  
Title: Contract #B26-04-001 Starlight Pavement Reconstruction - Phase 2 
Submitted For: William Flake, County Engineer 
Submitted By: Kathleen Outland, Procurement Officer
Department: Public Works
Presented By: William Flake
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of the award of Contract Number B26-04-001 for Starlight Pavement Reconstruction -
Phase 2 to Jud Co, LLC.
Background:
This is a pavement reconstruction project located in the Starlight Ridge Estates subdivision in District-V
of the Navajo County. The project includes portions of Gemini Lane, Carina Lane, Andromeda Way,
Luminary Way, Leo Lane, Aries Circle, Webb Drive and Starlight Ridge Road. The work consists of
removing existing pavement and subgrade, installing new geogrid, aggregate base course, 3 inches of
asphalt concrete pavement and other related work.
Sealed bids were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Governmental Center,
Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 PM M.S.T., May 28, 2026, in strict accordance with the specifications on
file. No bids were accepted after 3:00 PM M.S.T. The bids were publicly opened and read aloud at 3:15
PM. Seven bids were received and Jud Co, LLC was the lowest responsible, responsive bidder.
Recommendation: 
Staff is recommending award of Contract B26-04-001 Starlight Pavement Reconstruction - Phase 2 to Jud
Co, LLC.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2027 
Budgeted Y/N: Y 
Amount Requested: 1357922.23 
Fiscal Impact:
Funds are budgeted in fiscal year 2027 under the Capital Improvement Projects.

CONTRACT 
STARLIGHT PAVEMENT RECONSTRUCTION - PHASE 2, #B26-04-001 
 
THIS CONTRACT is made and entered into as of June 23, 2026 (the “Effective Date”) by and 
between Navajo County (“County”) and Jud Co, LLC (“Contractor”). 
 
1. Scope of Work:  
Contractor shall furnish any and all materials, equipment and labor required for 
performing all work for Starlight Pavement Reconstruction - Phase 2, #B26-04-
001, in accordance with the Contract Documents, in a workmanlike manner to the 
satisfaction of County through its Engineers. 
2. Contract Documents:  
The Contract Documents comprise this Contract and the attached ☒ Notice of 
Invitation for Bids, ☐ Speciﬁcations, ☒ General Provisions, ☐ Special Provisions, 
☒Proposal dated May 28, 2026, ☒ Bid Schedule, ☒ Surety (Bid) Bond, ☒ Statutory 
Performance Bond, ☒ Affidavit by Contractor Certifying That There Was No 
Collusion in Bidding for Contract, ☒ Intentions Concerning Subcontracting, ☒ 
Contractors Qualiﬁcations, ☒ Plans, and addenda thereto, if any.  If there is any 
conﬂict in the documents, this Contract shall control over all others; the 
Speciﬁcations shall control over the General Provisions and Special Provisions; and 
the Special Provisions shall control over the General Provisions. 
3. No Liens / Time for Completion:  
Contractor shall furnish all materials, equipment and labor in accordance with the 
Contract Documents free and clear of all claims, liens and charges whatsoever, 
within the time, or times, stated in the Proposal. 
4. Cancellation:  
This Contract is subject to cancellation pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-511 (concerning 
conﬂicts of interest). 
5. Payments:   
Payment shall be made on the basis of the unit price(s) bid for the awarded items in 
the Bidding Schedule for Contract #B26-04-001.  Contractor shall submit invoices to 
County for all work performed hereunder.  No later than 14 days after each invoice 
has been veriﬁed and approved by the Engineer, County shall pay to Contractor

ninety (90%) of the value of said work in place, as approved by the Engineer.  The 
balance of ten percent (10%) shall be retained by the Owner until the time of ﬁnal 
payment and acceptance of said work, as per A.R.S. §34-221.  The provisions of 
A.R.S. §34-221 are incorporated herein by this reference and shall control in regard 
to all matters of payment. 
6. Certiﬁcations: 
a. Prohibition of Boycott of Israel:  Contractor certiﬁes that neither it nor its

ethnic Uyghurs in the People’s Republic of China; and (3) any contractors, 
subcontractors, or suppliers that use the forced labor of ethnic Uyghurs in the

within one hundred eighty (180) after giving notice thereof, the same shall 
constitute a material breach of this Agreement, which shall then terminate 
automatically, except that if the Agreement termination date occurs before the 
end of the remedy period, this Agreement terminates on the Agreement’s 
termination date.  The County retains the legal right to inspect the records of the 
Contractor and all Subcontractors to ensure compliance with this certiﬁcation

Board of Supervisors Regular 10. b.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2026  
Title: Intergovernmental Agreement between Navajo County and Whippoorwill Chapter
Submitted By: Nicole Reske, Tribal Government Relations Liaison
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Nicole Reske
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of Intergovernmental Agreement between Navajo County and Whippoorwill Chapter.
Background:
Whippoorwill Chapter is responsible for supportive maintenance on public roads within the Whippoorwill
Chapter service area supporting school bus routes, emergency medical access and daily transportation for
residents of the Chapter. To ensure continued service, funding is needed for repairs on their backhoe,
which is needed for backfilling, assisting with culvert replacements and other routine maintenance. With
the support from Navajo County, Whippoorwill Chapter will be able to sustain their road grading efforts
and continue collaborative service alongside BIA and NDOT — ultimately benefiting the health, safety,
and mobility of the community members.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 27 
Budgeted Y/N:
Amount Requested: 15,000 
Fiscal Impact:

Navajo County- For Official Use Only
INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT
between the
COUNTY OF NAVAJO and  WHIPPOORWILL CHAPTER
concerning the MAINTENANCE OF INVENTORY ROUTES
THIS AGREEMENT is entered into as of June 23  rd  , 2026   (the 'Effective Date") by and 
between the NAVAJO COUNTY, a politician subdivision of the State of Arizona (the "County"),  
and the WHIPPOORWILL CHAPTER, a political subdivision of the Navajo Nation located within 
Navajo County and the Navajo Reservation (the "Chapter").
WHEREAS, the parties are authorized by A.R.S. §§ 11-951 and 11-952 to enter 
intergovernmental agreements for cooperative action; and
WHEREAS, A.R.S. § 11-251(4) authorizes the County to maintain all public roads within its 
boundaries; and
WHEREAS, A.R.S. § 11-251(29) authorizes the Board of Supervisor of the County to enter into 
agreements for the Indian lands, with the government of the United States acting through 
its duly authorized officers or agents pursuant to any act of Congress; and
WHEREAS, the above statues have been interpreted by the Arizona Attorney General in 
Opinion No. 186-029 to authorize the expenditure of funds for the maintenance of all public 
roads within Indian Country (as defined in 18 U.S.C. § 1151);
WHEREAS, the Code of Federal Regulations provides in 25 C.F.R. § 170.6 that "the 
administration and maintenance of Indian Reservation Roads and Bridges is basically a 
function of the local government. Subject to the availability of funds, the Commissioner (of 
Indian Affairs) shall maintain, or case to be maintained, those approved roads on the 
Federal-Aid Indian Road System;" and
WHEREAS, 25 C.F.R. § 170.7 provides that "The Commissioner may enter into an agreement 
with the State (which is defined to include political subdivisions such as maintenance of 
certain Indian Reservation Road and Bridges, especially at those locations where road 
projects serve non-Indian land as well as Indian land;" and
WHEREAS, the County has received from the Chapter a request for HURF funds to be 
used for heavy equipment repair for Chapter equipment which will result in the maintenance  
of Bureau of Indian Affairs (“BIA”) and Navajo Nation Division of Transportation (“NNDOT”) 
Public Routes serving the Chapter Community,
NOW, WHEREAS, pursuant to their authority as set forth above, the parties hereby agree  
as follows:

<!-- page 222 -->
Navajo County- For Official Use Only
1. Allocation     of     Highway     User     Revenue     Funds  . The Navajo County Board of 
Supervisors, by motion duly passed at its regular meeting on June 23  rd  , 2026   
approved the expenditure of up to $15,000.00 in HURF funds (the "Allocated 
Funds") to be used for the maintenance of Whippoorwill Chapter Heavy 
Equipment which maintains BIA Public Routes which are located on the Navajo 
Reservation in Navajo County, serving the school District and community.
2. Purposes.   The Chapter agrees that the Allocated Funds shall be used solely for 
the maintenance of Whippoorwill Chapter Equipment which maintains BIA public 
routes which are located on the Navajo Reservation in Navajo County, serve the 
Chapter and meet the HURF criteria. This may include the employment of 
personnel of contractors; the acquisition, operation, maintenance and repair of 
equipment; and the purchase of necessary materials and parts.
3. Disbursement     of     Allocated     Funds  . The allocated Funds shall be utilized to 
purchase material or parts by the County upon request by Whippoorwill 
Chapter.
4. Chapter Permission to Maintain Routes  . It is understood that the Chapter has 
an agreement set in place with Bureau of Indian Affairs (BIA) and Navajo Nation 
Division of Transportation (NDOT) to maintain roads within Chapter service 
area.
5. County Not Responsible for Maintenance.   The Chapter shall be responsible 
for identifying, prioritizing, scheduling and performing all maintenance activities 
for which the Allocated Funds are used in whole or part. It is understood that the  
County's sole obligation hereunder is to make payment of the Allocated Funds.
6. Term and     Termination.   This Agreement shall expire June 30, 2027 of its own 
accord when the Allocated Funds have been disbursed in their entirely. This 
Agreement may be terminated by any parties. This Agreement is subject to 
cancellation pursuant to A.R.S. 55 38-511.
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties have executed this Agreement as the Effective Date.
COUNTY OF NAVAJO
By                                                                                        
Daryl Seymore, Chairman, Board of Supervisors

 Leah Thomas, Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 11. a.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2026  
Title: Truth in Taxation Hearing
Submitted For: Jayson Vowell, Finance Director 
Submitted By: Jayson Vowell, Finance Director
Department: Administrative Services Division: Finance
Presented By: Jayson Vowell
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: TRUTH IN TAXATION pursuant to A.R.S. §42-17104 (primary) and §48-254
(secondary): Consideration and possible approval of property tax levy with no change to property tax
rates from the prior year. 
Background:
If the proposed primary and secondary property tax levy of the county and special taxing districts,
excluding amounts attributable to new construction, is greater than the amount levied in the preceding tax
year, a truth in taxation hearing is required.
Recommendation: 
This requires a roll call vote. Staff recommends approving the Truth in Taxation rates.

All interested citizens are invited to attend the public hearing on the
tax increase that is scheduled to be held Tuesday, June 23, 2026, at
9:00 AM, at the Navajo County Complex, 100 East Code Talkers
Drive, South Highway 77, Holbrook AZ, 86025.
Truth in Taxation Hearing
Notice of Tax Increase
In compliance with section 42-17107, Arizona Revised Statutes,
Navajo County is notifying its property taxpayers of Navajo County's
intention to raise its primary property taxes over last year's level.
Navajo County is proposing an increase in primary property taxes of
$199,960 or 2.20%.
For example, the proposed tax increase will cause Navajo County's
primary property taxes on a $100,000 home to be $81.14 (total
proposed taxes including tax increase). Without the proposed tax
increase, the total taxes that would be owed on a $100,000 home
would have been $79.39.
This proposed increase is exclusive of increased primary property
taxes received from new construction. The increase is also
exclusive of any changes that may occur from property tax levies for
voter approved bonded indebtedness or budget and tax overrides.

RESOLUTION   ___-26
RESOLUTION FOR THE ADOPTION OF THE BUDGET
FISCAL YEAR 2027
WHEREAS, in accordance with the provisions of Title 42, Chapter 17, Articles 1-5, Arizona 
Revised Statutes (A.R.S.), the Board of Supervisors did, on June 9, 2026, make an estimate of the 
different amounts required to meet the public expenditures/expenses for the ensuing year, also an 
estimate of revenues from sources other than direct taxation, and the amount to be raised by 
taxation upon real and personal property of Navajo County, and
WHEREAS, in accordance with said chapter of said title, and following due public notice, the Board 
met on June 23, 2026, at which meeting any taxpayer was privileged to appear and be heard in favor 
of or against any of the proposed expenditures/expenses or tax levies, and
WHEREAS, it appears that publication has been duly made as required by law, of said estimates 
together with a notice that the Board would meet on June 23, 2026, at the office of the Board for the 
purpose of hearing taxpayers and making tax levies as set forth in said estimates, and
WHEREAS, it appears that the sums to be raised by taxation, as specified therein, do not in the 
aggregate exceed that amount as computed in A.R.S. §42-17051(A).
THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the said estimates of revenues and expenditures/expenses 
shown on the accompanying schedules, as now increased, reduced, or changed, are hereby adopted as 
the budget of Navajo County for the fiscal year 2026-2027.
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors on this 
23rd day of June 2026.
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
By ________________________________________
Daryl Seymore, Chairman of the Board
ATTEST:

Leah Thomas, Clerk of the Board

Background:
As required by statute, a full and complete statement of the County's financial affairs for the preceding
fiscal year are included.  Additionally, an estimate of the amounts required to meet the County's
expenditures of Fiscal Year 2026-27 is included and has been presented on a summary schedule of
estimated revenues and expenditures in accordance with the Arizona Office of the Auditor General's
prescribed format.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends Final Adoption of the Fiscal Year 2026-27 budgets for all districts included in the
County's budget documents.
